Syntax and Typing of
\lambda^{E}
.

B.2.
Constant Property

Theorem B.1.

For the constants
c=\texttt{true},\texttt{false},\texttt{unit},
and
==_{b}
, Property
1
holds, i.e.,
c\in\left[\!\left|\texttt{TyCon}(c)\right|\!\right]
.

Proof.

Below are the proofs for each of the four constants.

•

e\equiv\texttt{true}
and
e\in\left[\!\left|\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{Bool}}\mid{x==_{\texttt{Bool}}\texttt{true}}\}\right|\!\right]
.
We need to prove the below three requirements of membership in the interpretation of basic types:

–

e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
, which holds because
true
is a value, thus
v=\texttt{true}
;

–

\vdash_{B}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{Bool}
, which holds by the typing rule
BTCon
; and

–

(x==_{\texttt{Bool}}\texttt{true})[e/x]\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
, which holds because

\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x==_{\texttt{Bool}}\texttt{true})[e/x]&=&\texttt{true}==_{\texttt{Bool}}\texttt{true}\\
&\hookrightarrow&(==_{(\texttt{true},\texttt{Bool})})\ \texttt{true}\\
&\hookrightarrow&\texttt{true}=\texttt{true}\\
&=&\texttt{true}\end{array}

•

e\equiv\texttt{false}
and
e\in\left[\!\left|\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{Bool}}\mid{x==_{\texttt{Bool}}\texttt{false}}\}\right|\!\right]
.
We need to prove the below three requirements of membership in the interpretation of basic types:

–

e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
, which holds because
false
is a value, thus
v=\texttt{false}
;

–

\vdash_{B}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{Bool}
, which holds by the typing rule
BTCon
; and

–

(x==_{\texttt{Bool}}\texttt{false})[e/x]\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
, which holds because

\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x==_{\texttt{Bool}}\texttt{false})[e/x]&=&\texttt{false}==_{\texttt{Bool}}\texttt{false}\\
&\hookrightarrow&(==_{(\texttt{false},\texttt{Bool})})\ \texttt{false}\\
&\hookrightarrow&\texttt{false}=\texttt{false}\\
&=&\texttt{true}\end{array}

•

e\equiv\texttt{unit}
and
e\in\left[\!\left|\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{x==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit}}\}\right|\!\right]
.
We need to prove the below three requirements of membership in the interpretation of basic types:

–

e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
, which holds because
unit
is a value, thus
v=\texttt{unit}
;

–

\vdash_{B}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{()}
, which holds by the typing rule
BTCon
; and

–

(x==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit})[e/x]\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
, which holds because

\begin{array}[]{rcl}(x==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit})[e/x]&=&\texttt{unit}==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit}\\
&\hookrightarrow&(==_{(\texttt{unit},\texttt{()})})\ \texttt{unit}\\
&\hookrightarrow&\texttt{unit}=\texttt{unit}\\
&=&\texttt{true}\end{array}

•

==_{b}\in\left[\!\left|{x}\text{:}{b}\rightarrow{y}\text{:}{b}\rightarrow\{{z}\text{:}{\texttt{Bool}}\mid{z==_{\texttt{Bool}}(x==_{b}y)}\}\right|\!\right]
.
By the definition of interpretation of function types, we fix

e_{x},e_{y}\in\left[\!\left|b\right|\!\right]
and we need to prove that

e\equiv e_{x}==_{b}e_{y}\in\left[\!\left|(\{{z}\text{:}{\texttt{Bool}}\mid{z==_{\texttt{Bool}}(x==_{b}y)}\})[e_{x}/x][e_{y}/y]\right|\!\right]
.
We prove the below three requirements of membership in the interpretation of basic types:

–

e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
, which holds because

\begin{array}[]{rcll}e&=&e_{x}==_{b}e_{y}&\\
&\hookrightarrow^{*}&v_{x}==_{b}e_{y}&\text{because }e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|b\right|\!\right]\\
&\hookrightarrow^{*}&v_{x}==_{b}v_{y}&\text{because }e_{y}\in\left[\!\left|b\right|\!\right]\\
&\hookrightarrow&(==_{(v_{x},b)})\ v_{y}&\\
&\hookrightarrow&v_{x}=v_{y}&\\
&=&v&\text{with }v=\texttt{true}\text{ or }v=\texttt{false}\end{array}

–

\vdash_{B}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{Bool}
, which holds by the typing rule
BTCon
and because

e_{x},e_{y}\in\left[\!\left|b\right|\!\right]
thus
\vdash_{B}e_{x}\,\mathrel{::}\,b
and
\vdash_{B}e_{y}\,\mathrel{::}\,b
; and

–

(z==_{\texttt{Bool}}(x==_{b}y))[e/z][e_{x}/x][e_{y}/y]\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
. Since
e_{x},e_{y}\in\left[\!\left|b\right|\!\right]

both expressions evaluate to values,
say
e_{x}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{x}
and
e_{y}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{y}

which holds because

\begin{array}[]{rcll}(z==_{\texttt{Bool}}(x==_{b}y))[e/z][e_{x}/x][e_{y}/y]&=&e==_{\texttt{Bool}}(e_{x}==_{b}e_{y})&\\
&=&(e_{x}==_{b}e_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}(e_{x}==_{b}e_{y})&\\
&\hookrightarrow^{*}&(v_{x}==_{b}e_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}(e_{x}==_{b}e_{y})&\text{since }e_{x}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{x}\\
&\hookrightarrow^{*}&(v_{x}==_{b}v_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}(e_{x}==_{b}e_{y})&\text{since }e_{y}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{y}\\
&\hookrightarrow&((==_{(v_{x},b)})\ v_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}(e_{x}==_{b}e_{y})&\\
&\hookrightarrow&(v_{x}=v_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}(e_{x}==_{b}e_{y})&\\
&\hookrightarrow^{*}&(v_{x}=v_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}(v_{x}==_{b}e_{y})&\text{since }e_{x}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{x}\\
&\hookrightarrow^{*}&(v_{x}=v_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}(v_{x}==_{b}v_{y})&\text{since }e_{y}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{y}\\
&\hookrightarrow&(v_{x}=v_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}((==_{(v_{x},b)})\ v_{y})&\\
&\hookrightarrow&(v_{x}=v_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}(v_{x}=v_{y})&\\
&\hookrightarrow&(v_{x}=v_{y})==_{\texttt{Bool}}(v_{x}=v_{y})&\\
&\hookrightarrow&((==_{((v_{x}=v_{y}),\texttt{Bool})})\ (v_{x}=v_{y})&\\
&\hookrightarrow&(v_{x}=v_{y})=(v_{x}=v_{y})&\\
&=&\texttt{true}&\\
\end{array}

∎

B.3.
Type Soundness

Theorem B.2 (Semantic soundness).

If
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau
then
\Gamma\models e\in\tau
.

Proof.

By induction on the typing derivation.

TSub

By inversion of the rule we have

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau^{\prime}

(2)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\tau}

By IH on (1) we have

(3)

\Gamma\models e\in\tau^{\prime}

By Theorem
B.6
and (2) we have

(4)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}}\ \subseteq\ {\tau}

By (3), (4), and the definition of subsets we directly get
\Gamma\models e\in\tau
.

TSelf

Assume
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{z==_{b}e}\}
.
By inversion we have

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}

By IH we have

(2)

\Gamma\models e\in\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}

We fix
\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma\right|\!\right]
. By the definition of semantic typing we get

(3)

{\theta}\cdot{e}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}}\right|\!\right]

By the definition of denotations on basic types we have

(4)

{\theta}\cdot{e}\hookrightarrow^{*}v

(5)

\vdash_{B}{\theta}\cdot{e}\,\mathrel{::}\,b

(6)

{\theta}\cdot{r}[{\theta}\cdot{e}/z]\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

Since
\theta
contains values, by the definition of
==_{b}
we have

(7)

{\theta}\cdot{e}==_{b}{\theta}\cdot{e}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

Thus

(8)

{\theta}\cdot{(z==_{b}e)}[{\theta}\cdot{e}/z]\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

By (4), (5), and (8) we have

(9)

{\theta}\cdot{e}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{z==_{b}e}\}}\right|\!\right]

Thus,
\Gamma\models e\in\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{z==_{b}e}\}
.

TCon

This case holds exactly because of Property
B.1
.

TVar

This case holds by the definition of closing substitutions.

TLam

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x}.~{}e\mathrel{\text{::}}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau
.
By inversion of the rule we have

\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau
.
By IH we get

\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}\models e\in\tau
.

We need to show that
\Gamma\models\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x}.~{}e\in{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau
.
Which, for some
\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma\right|\!\right]
is equivalent to

\lambda x\mathord{:}{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{x}}.~{}{\theta}\cdot{e}

\in

\left[\!\left|{x}\text{:}{{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{x}}}\rightarrow{\theta}\cdot{\tau}\right|\!\right]
.

We pick a random
e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{x}}\right|\!\right]
thus
we need to show that

{\theta}\cdot{e[e_{x}/x]}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau[e_{x}/x]}\right|\!\right]
.
By Lemma
B.3
, there exists
v_{x}
so that

e_{x}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{x}
and
v_{x}\in\left[\!\left|\tau_{x}\right|\!\right]
.
By the inductive hypothesis,

{\theta}\cdot{e[v_{x}/x]}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau[v_{x}/x]}\right|\!\right]
.
By Lemma
B.4
,

{\theta}\cdot{e[e_{x}/x]}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau[e_{x}/x]}\right|\!\right]
,
which concludes our proof.

TApp

Assume
\Gamma\vdash e\ e_{x}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau[e_{x}/x]
.
By inversion we have

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau

(2)

\Gamma\vdash e_{x}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{x}

By IH we get

(3)

\Gamma\models e\in{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau

(4)

\Gamma\models e_{x}\in\tau_{x}

We fix
\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma\right|\!\right]
.
By the definition of semantic types

(5)

{\theta}\cdot{e}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\right|\!\right]

(6)

{\theta}\cdot{e_{x}}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{x}}\right|\!\right]

By (5), (6), and the definition of semantic typing on functions:

(7)

{\theta}\cdot{e\ e_{x}}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau[e_{x}/x]}\right|\!\right]

Which directly leads to the required

\Gamma\models e\ e_{x}\in\tau[e_{x}/x]

TEqBase

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{b}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
.
By inversion we get:

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e_{l}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{l}

(2)

\Gamma\vdash e_{r}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{r}

(3)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{l}}\ \preceq\ {\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{\texttt{true}}\}}

(4)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{r}}\ \preceq\ {\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{\texttt{true}}\}}

(5)

\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}

By IH we get

(4)

\Gamma\models e_{l}\in\tau_{l}

(5)

\Gamma\models e_{r}\in\tau_{r}

(6)

\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\models e\in\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}

We fix
\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma\right|\!\right]
. Then (4) and (5) become

(7)

{\theta}\cdot{e_{l}}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{l}}\right|\!\right]

(8)

{\theta}\cdot{e_{r}}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{r}}\right|\!\right]

(9)

\Gamma\models e_{r}\in\tau_{r}

(10)

\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\models e\in\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}

Assume

(11)

{\theta}\cdot{e_{l}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{l}

(12)

{\theta}\cdot{e_{r}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{r}

By (7), (8), (11), (12), and Lemma
B.3
we get

(13)

v_{l}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{l}}\right|\!\right]

(14)

v_{r}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{r}}\right|\!\right]

By (10), (11), and (12) we get

(15)

v_{l}==_{b}v_{r}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

By (11), (12), (15), ane Lemma
B.5
we have

(16)

{\theta}\cdot{e_{l}}==_{b}{\theta}\cdot{e_{r}}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

By (1-5) we get:

(17)

\vdash_{B}{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{PBEq}_{b}

Trivially, with zero evaluation steps we have:

(18)

{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ ({{\theta}\cdot{e_{l}}})\ ({{\theta}\cdot{e_{l}}})\ ({{\theta}\cdot{e}})

By (16), (17), (18) and the definition of semantic types on basic equality types we have

(19)

{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{PEq}_{b}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\right|\!\right]

Which leads to the required

\Gamma\models\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\in\texttt{PEq}_{b}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
.

TEqFun

Assume
\Gamma\vdash\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
.
By inversion we have

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e_{l}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{l}

(2)

\Gamma\vdash e_{r}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{r}

(3)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{l}}\ \preceq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}

(4)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{r}}\ \preceq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}

(5)

\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}({x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\})

(6)

\Gamma\vdash{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau

By IH and Theorem
B.6
we get

(7)

\Gamma\models e_{l}\in\tau_{l}

(8)

\Gamma\models e_{r}\in\tau_{r}

(9)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{l}}\ \subseteq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}

(10)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{r}}\ \subseteq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}

(11)

\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\models e\in({x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\})

By (1-5) we get

(12)

\vdash_{B}{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{PBEq}_{\lfloor{\theta}\cdot{({x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau)}\rfloor}

Trivially, by zero evaluation steps, we get

(13)

{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{x}}}\rightarrow{\theta}\cdot{\tau}}\ ({{\theta}\cdot{e_{l}}})\ ({\theta}\cdot{e_{r}})\ ({\theta}\cdot{e})

By (7-10) we get

(14)

{\theta}\cdot{e_{l}},{\theta}\cdot{e_{r}}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\right|\!\right]

By (7), (8), (11), the definition of semantic types on functions, and Lemmata
B.3
and
B.4

(similar to the previous case) we have

–

\forall e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|\tau_{x}\right|\!\right].e\ e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|\texttt{PEq}_{\tau[e_{x}/x]}\ \{e_{l}\ e_{x}\}\ \{e_{r}\ e_{x}\}\right|\!\right]

By (12), (13), (14), and (15) we get

(19)

{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}

\in

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\right|\!\right]

Which leads to the required

\Gamma\models\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\in\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
.

∎

Lemma B.3.

If
e\in\left[\!\left|\tau\right|\!\right]
, then
e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
and
v\in\left[\!\left|\tau\right|\!\right]
.

Proof.

By structural induction of the type
\tau
.
∎

Lemma B.4.

If
e_{x}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{x}
and
e[v_{x}/x]\in\left[\!\left|\tau[v_{x}/x]\right|\!\right]
,
then
e[e_{x}/x]\in\left[\!\left|\tau[e_{x}/x]\right|\!\right]
.

Proof.

We can use parallel reductions (of §
C
) to prove that if

e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
,
then (1)
\left[\!\left|\tau[e_{1}/x]\right|\!\right]=\left[\!\left|\tau[e_{2}/x]\right|\!\right]
and
(2)
e_{1}\in\left[\!\left|\tau\right|\!\right]

iff

e_{2}\in\left[\!\left|\tau\right|\!\right]
.
The proof directly follows by these two properties.
∎

Lemma B.5.

If
e_{x}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{x}^{\prime}
and
e[e_{x}^{\prime}/x]\hookrightarrow^{*}c
,
then
e[e_{x}/x]\hookrightarrow^{*}c
.

Proof.

As an instance of Corollary
C.17
.
∎

We define semantic subtyping as follows:

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau}\ \subseteq\ {\tau^{\prime}}

iff

\forall\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma\right|\!\right].\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau}\right|\!\right]\subseteq\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau^{\prime}}\right|\!\right]
.

Theorem B.6 (Subtyping semantic soundness).

If
{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}
then
{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau}\ \subseteq\ {\tau^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation tree:

SBase

Assume
{\Gamma}\vdash{\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}}\ \preceq\ {\{{x^{\prime}}\text{:}{b}\mid{r^{\prime}}\}}
.
By inversion

\forall\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma\right|\!\right],~{}\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}}\right|\!\right]\subseteq\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x^{\prime}}\text{:}{b}\mid{r^{\prime}}\}}\right|\!\right]
, which exactly leads to the required.

SFun

Assume
{\Gamma}\vdash{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \preceq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}
.
By inversion

(1)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{x}}

(2)

{\Gamma,x:\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\vdash{\tau}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}

By IH

(3)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\ \subseteq\ {\tau_{x}}

(4)

{\Gamma,x:\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\vdash{\tau}\ \subseteq\ {\tau^{\prime}}

We fix
\theta\in\Gamma
.
We pick
e
. We assume
e\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\right|\!\right]

and we will show that
e\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\right|\!\right]
.
By assumption

(5)

\forall e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{x}}\right|\!\right]\!.\ e\ e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau[e_{x}/x]}\right|\!\right]

We need to show

\forall e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\right|\!\right]\!.\ e\ e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau^{\prime}[e_{x}/x]}\right|\!\right]
.
We fix
e_{x}
.
By (3), if
e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\right|\!\right]
, then

e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{x}}\right|\!\right]
and (5) applies, so

e\ e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau[e_{x}/x]}\right|\!\right]
, which by (4) gives

e\ e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau^{\prime}[e_{x}/x]}\right|\!\right]
.
Thus,
e\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\right|\!\right]
.
This leads to
\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\right|\!\right]\subseteq\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\right|\!\right]
,
which by definition gives semantic subtyping:

{\Gamma}\vdash{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \subseteq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}
.

SEq

Assume
{\Gamma}\vdash{\texttt{PEq}_{\tau_{i}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\ \preceq\ {\texttt{PEq}_{\tau_{i}^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}
.
We split cases on the structure of
\tau_{i}
.

–

If

\tau_{i}
is a basic type, then
\tau_{i}
is trivially refined to true.
Thus,
\tau_{i}=\tau_{i}^{\prime}=b
and
for each
\theta\in\Gamma
,
\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\right|\!\right]=\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\right|\!\right]
,
thus set inclusion reduces to equal sets.

–

If

\tau_{i}
is a function type, thus

{\Gamma}\vdash{\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\ \preceq\ {\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}

By inversion

(1)

{\Gamma}\vdash{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \preceq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}

(2)

{\Gamma}\vdash{{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}

By inversion on (1) and (2) we get

(3)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{x}}

(4)

{\Gamma,x:\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\vdash{\tau}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}

(5)

{\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\tau}

By IH on (1) and (3) we get

(6)

{\Gamma}\vdash{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \subseteq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}

(7)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\ \subseteq\ {\tau_{x}}

We fix
\theta\in\Gamma
and some
e
.
If
e\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\right|\!\right]

we need to show that

e\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\right|\!\right]
.
By the assumption we have

(8)

\vdash_{B}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{PBEq}_{\lfloor{\theta}\cdot{({x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau)}\rfloor}

(9)

e\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{xEq}_{\_}\ ({\theta}\cdot{e_{l}})\ ({\theta}\cdot{e_{r}})\ {e_{pf}}

(10)

({\theta}\cdot{e_{l}}),({\theta}\cdot{e_{r}})\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{(}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau)\right|\!\right]

(11)

\forall e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau_{x}}\right|\!\right].e_{pf}\ e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|\texttt{PEq}_{{\theta}\cdot{(}\tau[e_{x}/x])}\ \{({\theta}\cdot{e_{l}})\ e_{x}\}\ \{({\theta}\cdot{e_{r}})\ e_{x}\}\right|\!\right]

Since (8) only depends on the structure of the type index, we get

(12)

\vdash_{B}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{PBEq}_{\lfloor{\theta}\cdot{({x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime})}\rfloor}

By (6) and (10) we get

(13)

({\theta}\cdot{e_{l}}),({\theta}\cdot{e_{r}})\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{(}{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime})\right|\!\right]

By (4), (5), Lemma
B.7
, the rule
SEq
and the IH, we get that

\left[\!\left|\texttt{PEq}_{{\theta}\cdot{(}\tau[e_{x}/x])}\ \{({\theta}\cdot{e_{l}})\ e_{x}\}\ \{({\theta}\cdot{e_{r}})\ e_{x}\}\right|\!\right]\subseteq\left[\!\left|\texttt{PEq}_{{\theta}\cdot{(}\tau^{\prime}[e_{x}/x])}\ \{({\theta}\cdot{e_{l}})\ e_{x}\}\ \{({\theta}\cdot{e_{r}})\ e_{x}\}\right|\!\right]
.
By which, (11), (7), and reasoning similar to the
SFun
case, we get

(14)

\forall e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\right|\!\right].e_{pf}\ e_{x}\in\left[\!\left|\texttt{PEq}_{{\theta}\cdot{(}\tau^{\prime}[e_{x}/x])}\ \{({\theta}\cdot{e_{l}})\ e_{x}\}\ \{({\theta}\cdot{e_{r}})\ e_{x}\}\right|\!\right]

By (12), (9), (13), and (14) we conclude that

e\in\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\right|\!\right]
,
thus

{\Gamma}\vdash{\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\ \subseteq\ {\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}
.

∎

Lemma B.7 (Strengthening).

If
{\Gamma_{1}}\vdash{\tau_{1}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{2}}
, then:

(1)

If
\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{2},\Gamma_{2}\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau

then
\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau
.

(2)

If
{\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{2},\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\tau}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}

then
{\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{1},\Gamma_{2}}\vdash{\tau}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}
.

(3)

If
\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{2},\Gamma_{2}\vdash\tau

then
\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash\tau
.

(4)

If
\vdash\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{2},\Gamma_{2}

then
\vdash\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{1},\Gamma_{2}
.

Proof.

The proofs go by induction.
Only the
TVar
case is insteresting; we use
TSub
and our assumption.

∎

Lemma B.8 (Semantic typing is closed under parallel reduction in expressions).

If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows^{*}e_{2}
, then
e_{1}\in\left[\!\left|\tau\right|\!\right]
iff
e_{2}\in\left[\!\left|\tau\right|\!\right]
.

Proof.

By induction on
\tau
, using parallel reduction as a bisimulation
(Lemma
C.5
and
Corollary
C.15
).
∎

Lemma B.9 (Semantic typing is closed under parallel reduction in types).

If
\tau_{1}\rightrightarrows^{*}\tau_{2}
then
\left[\!\left|\tau_{1}\right|\!\right]=\left[\!\left|\tau_{2}\right|\!\right]
.

Proof.

By induction on
\tau_{1}
(which necessarily has the same shape as

\tau_{2}
). We use parallel reduction as a bisimulation
(Lemma
C.5
and
Corollary
C.15
).
∎

Lemma B.10 (Parallel reducing types are equal).

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}
and
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}
and

\tau_{1}\rightrightarrows^{*}\tau_{2}
then
{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{1}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{2}}
and

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{1}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{2}}
.

Proof.

By induction on the parallel reduction sequence; for a single step,
by induction on
\tau_{1}
(which must have the same structure as

\tau_{2}
).
We use parallel reduction as a bisimulation
(Lemma
C.5
and
Corollary
C.15
).
∎

Lemma B.11 (Regularity).

(1)

If
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau
then
\vdash\Gamma
and
\Gamma\vdash\tau
.

(2)

If
\Gamma\vdash\tau
then
\vdash\Gamma
.

(3)

If
{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{1}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{2}}
then
\vdash\Gamma
and
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}
and
\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}
.

Proof.

By a big ol’ induction.

∎

Lemma B.12 (Canonical forms).

If
\Gamma\vdash v\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau
, then:

•

If
\tau=\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{e}\}
, then
v=c
such
that
\texttt{TyCon}(c)=b
and

{\Gamma}\vdash{\texttt{TyCon}(c)}\ \preceq\ {\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{e}\}}
.

•

If
\tau={x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
, then
v=\textsc{TLam}{x}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}{e}
such that

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{x}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{x}^{\prime}}
and
\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}^{\prime}\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau^{\prime\prime}
such that
{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {.}

•

If
\tau=\texttt{PEq}_{b}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
then
v=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ v_{p}
such that

\Gamma\vdash e_{l}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{l}
and

\Gamma\vdash e_{r}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{r}
(for some
\tau_{l}
and
\tau_{r}

that are refinements of
b
) and
\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\vdash v_{p}\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}
.

•

If
\tau=\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
then
v=\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime\prime}}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ v_{p}
such that

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{x}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{x}^{\prime}}
and
{\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}}\vdash{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}
and

\Gamma\vdash e_{l}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{l}
and

\Gamma\vdash e_{r}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{r}
(for some
\tau_{l}
and
\tau_{r}

that are subtypes of
{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime\prime}
) and
\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\vdash v_{p}\mathrel{\text{::}}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\ x\}\ \{e_{r}\ x\}
.

B.4.
The Binary Logical Relation

Theorem B.13 (
\mathsf{EqRT}
soundness).

If
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
,
then
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.

Proof.

By
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
and
the Fundamental Property
B.22
we have

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\sim}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
.
Thus, for a fixed
\delta\in\Gamma
,

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\};{\delta}
.
By the definition of the logical relation for
EqRT
, we have

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.
So,
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.
∎

Lemma B.14 (LR respects subtyping).

If
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}
,
then
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of the subtyping tree.

SBase

By assumption we have

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}

(2)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}}\ \preceq\ {\{{x^{\prime}}\text{:}{b}\mid{r^{\prime}}\}}

By inversion on (2) we get

(3)

\forall\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma\right|\!\right],~{}\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}}\right|\!\right]\subseteq\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x^{\prime}}\text{:}{b}\mid{r^{\prime}}\}}\right|\!\right]

We fix
\delta\in\Gamma
.
By (1) we get

(4)

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\mathrel{::}\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\};{\delta}

By the definition of logical relations:

(5)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}

(6)

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}

(7)

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\};{\delta}

By (7) and the definition of the logical relation on basic types we have

(8)

v_{1}=v_{2}=c

(9)

\vdash_{B}c\,\mathrel{::}\,b

(10)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{r[c/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

(11)

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{r[c/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

By (3), (10) and (11) become

(12)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{r^{\prime}[c/x^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

(13)

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{r^{\prime}[c/x^{\prime}]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

By (8), (9), (12), and (13) we get

(14)

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\{{x^{\prime}}\text{:}{b}\mid{r^{\prime}}\};{\delta}

By (5), (6), and (14) we have

(15)

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\mathrel{::}\{{x^{\prime}}\text{:}{b}\mid{r^{\prime}}\};{\delta}

Thus,
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\{{x^{\prime}}\text{:}{b}\mid{r^{\prime}}\}
.

SFun

By assumption:

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau

(2)

{\Gamma}\vdash{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \preceq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}

By inversion of the rule (2)

(3)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{x}}

(4)

{\Gamma,x:\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\vdash{\tau}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}

We fix
\delta\in\Gamma
.
By (1) and the definition of logical relation

(5)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}

(6)

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}

(7)

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau;{\delta}

We fix
v^{\prime}_{1}
and
v^{\prime}_{2}
so that

(8)

v^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime}_{x};{\delta}

By (8) and the definition of logical relations, since the values are idempotent under substitution,
we have

(9)

\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau^{\prime}_{x}

By (9) and inductive hypothesis on (3) we have

(10)

\Gamma\vdash v^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau_{x}

By (10), idempotence of values under substitution,
and the definition of logical relations, we have

(11)

v^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau_{x};{\delta}

By (7), (11), and the definition of logical relations on function values:

(12)

v_{1}\ v^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\ v^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta,(v^{\prime}_{1},v^{\prime}_{2})/x}

By (9), (12), and the definition of logical relations we have

(12)

\Gamma,x:\tau^{\prime}_{x}\vdash v_{1}\ v^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\ v^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau

By (12) and inductive hypothesis on (4) we have

(13)

\Gamma,x:\tau^{\prime}_{x}\vdash v_{1}\ v^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\ v^{\prime}_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau^{\prime}

By (8), (13), and the definition of logical relations, we have

(14)

v_{1}\ v^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\ v^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta,(v^{\prime}_{1},v^{\prime}_{2})/x}

By (8), (14), and the definition of logical relations, we have

(15)

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime};{\delta}

By (5), (6), and (15), we get

(16)

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\mathrel{::}{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime};{\delta}

So,
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
.

SEq

By hypothesis:

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}

(2)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}\ \preceq\ {\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}}

We fix
\delta\in\Gamma
.
By (1)

(3)

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\delta}

By (3) and the definition of logical relations.

(4)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}

(5)

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}

(6)

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\delta}

By (6) and the definition of logical relations

(7)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{l}}\mathrel{\sim}{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{r}}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}

By (7) and the definition of logical relations.

(8)

\Gamma\vdash{e_{l}}\mathrel{\sim}{e_{r}}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau

By inversion on (2)

(9)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}

(10)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\tau}

By (8) and inductive hypothesis on (9)

(11)

\Gamma\vdash{e_{l}}\mathrel{\sim}{e_{r}}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau^{\prime}

Thus,

(12)

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{{e_{l}}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{{e_{r}}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta}

By (12), (4), (5), and determinism of operational semantics:

(12)

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\delta}

By (4), (5), and (13)

(14)

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\delta}

So, by definition of logical relations,

\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
.

∎

Lemma B.15 (Constant soundness).

\Gamma\vdash c\mathrel{\sim}c\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{TyCon}(c)

Proof.

The proof follows the same steps as Theorem
B.1
.
∎

Lemma B.16 (Selfification of constants).

If
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\sim}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}

then
\Gamma\vdash x\mathrel{\sim}x\,\mathrel{::}\,\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{z==_{b}x}\}
.

Proof.

We fix
\delta\in\Gamma
.
By hypothesis
(v_{1},v_{2})/x\in\delta
with

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\};{\delta}
.
We need to show that

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{x}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{x}\mathrel{::}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{z==_{b}x}\};{\delta}
.
Which reduces to

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{z==_{b}x}\};{\delta}
.
By the definition on the logical relation on basic values, we know

v_{1}=v_{2}=c
and
\vdash_{B}c\,\mathrel{::}\,b
.
Thus, we are left to prove that

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{((z==_{b}x)[c/z])}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
and

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{((z==_{b}x)[c/z])}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

which, both, trivially hold by the definition of
==_{b}
.
∎

Lemma B.17 (Variable soundness).

If
x:\tau\in\Gamma
,
then
\Gamma\vdash x\mathrel{\sim}x\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.

Proof.

By the definition of the logical relation it suffices to show that

\forall\delta\in\Gamma.\delta_{1}(x)\mathrel{\sim}\delta_{2}(x)\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
;
which is trivially true by the definition of
\delta\in\Gamma
.
∎

Lemma B.18 (Transitivity of Evaluation).

If
e\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}
, then

e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
iff
e^{\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}v
.

Proof.

Assume
e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
.
Since the
\hookrightarrow
is by definition deterministic,
there exists a unique sequence

e\hookrightarrow e_{1}\hookrightarrow\dots\hookrightarrow e_{i}\hookrightarrow\dots\hookrightarrow v
.
By assumption,
e\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}
, so there exists a
j
, so
e^{\prime}\equiv e_{j}
,
and
e^{\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}v
following the same sequence.

Assume
e^{\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}v
.
Then
e\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}v
uniquely evaluates
e
to
v
.
∎

Lemma B.19 (LR closed under evaluation).

If
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}_{1}
,

e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}_{2}
, then

e^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}

iff
e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.

Proof.

Assume
e^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
, by the definition of
the logical relation on closed terms we have

e^{\prime}_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}
,
e^{\prime}_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}
,
and
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.
By Lemma
B.18
and
by assumption,
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}_{1}
and
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}_{2}
,
we have
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}
and
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}
.
By which and
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
we get that

e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.
The other direction is identical.
∎

Lemma B.20 (LR closed under parallel reduction).

If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows^{*}e^{\prime}_{1}
,

e_{2}\rightrightarrows^{*}e^{\prime}_{2}
, and

e^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e^{\prime}_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
,
then
e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.

Proof.

By induction on
\tau
, using parallel reduction as a backward
simulation (Corollary
C.15
).

∎

Lemma B.21 (LR Compositionality).

If
{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{x}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{x_{1}}
,

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{x}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{x_{2}}
,

e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta,(v_{x_{1}},v_{x_{2}})/x}
,
then
e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau[e_{x}/x];{\delta}
.

Proof.

By the assumption we have that

(1)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{x}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{x_{1}}

(2)

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{x}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{x_{2}}

(3)

e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}

(4)

e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}

(5)

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta,(v_{x1},v_{x_{2}})/x}

and we need to prove that

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau[e_{x}/x];{\delta}
.
The proof goes by structural induction on the type
\tau
.

•

\tau\doteq\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}
.
For
i=1,2
we need to show that
if
{\delta_{i},[v_{x_{i}}/x]}\cdot{r[v_{i}/z]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

then
{\delta_{i}}\cdot{r[v_{i}/z][e_{i}/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
.
We have
{\delta_{i},[v_{x_{i}}/x]}\cdot{r[v_{i}/z]}\rightrightarrows^{*}{\delta_{i}}\cdot{r[v_{i}/z][e_{i}/x]}

because substituting parallel reducing terms parallel reduces
(Corollary
C.3
) and parallel reduction
subsumes reduction (Lemma
C.4
).
By cotermination at constants (Corollary
C.17
), we have

{\delta_{i}}\cdot{r[v_{i}/z][e_{i}/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
.

•

\tau\doteq{y}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{y}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
.
We need to show that
if
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}{y}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{y}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime};{\delta,(v_{x_{1}},v_{x_{2}})/x}
,
then
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}{y}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{y}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}[e_{x}/x];{\delta}
.

We fix
v_{y_{1}}
and
v_{y_{2}}
so that

v_{y_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{y_{2}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime}_{y};{\delta,(v_{x_{1}},v_{x_{2}})/x}
.

Then, we have that

v_{1}\ v_{y_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\ v_{y_{2}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta,(v_{x_{1}},v_{x_{2}})/x,(v_{y_{1}},v_{y_{2}})/y}
.

By inductive hypothesis, we have that

v_{1}\ v_{y_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\ v_{y_{2}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime}[e_{x}/x];{\delta,(v_{y_{1}},v_{y_{2}})/y}
.

By inductive hypothesis on the fixed arguments, we also get

v_{y_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{y_{2}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime}_{y}[e_{x}/x];{\delta}
.

Combined, we get
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}{y}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{y}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}[e_{x}/x];{\delta}
.

•

\tau\doteq\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
.
We need to show that
if
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\delta,(v_{x_{1}},v_{x_{2}})/x}
,
then
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}[e_{x}/x];{\delta}
.

This reduces to showing that
if
{\delta_{1},[v_{x_{1}}/x]}\cdot{e_{l}}\mathrel{\sim}{\delta_{2},[v_{x_{2}}/x]}\cdot{e_{r}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta}
,
then
{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{l}[e_{x}/x]}\mathrel{\sim}{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{r}[e_{x}/x]}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta}
;
we find

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{l}[e_{x}/x]}\rightrightarrows^{*}{\delta_{1},[v_{x_{1}}/x]}\cdot{e_{l}}

and

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{r}[e_{x}/x]}\rightrightarrows^{*}{\delta_{2},[v_{x_{2}}/x]}\cdot{e_{r}}

because substituting multiple parallel reduction is parallel reduction (Corollary
C.3
).
The logical relation is closed under parallel reduction (Lemma
B.20
), and so

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{l}[e_{x}/x]}\mathrel{\sim}{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{r}[e_{x}/x]}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta}
.

∎

Theorem B.22 (LR Fundamental Property).

If
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau
, then

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\sim}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.

Proof.

The proof goes by induction on the derivation tree:

TSub

By inversion of the rule we have

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau^{\prime}

(2)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\tau}

By IH on (1) we have

(3)

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\sim}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau^{\prime}

By (3), (4), and Lemma
B.14
we have

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\sim}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.

TCon

By Lemma
B.15
.

TSelf

By inversion of the rule, we have:

(1)

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}
.

(2)

By the IH on (
1
), we have:

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\sim}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}
.

(3)

We fix a
\delta
such that:

\delta\in\Gamma
and

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e}\mathrel{\sim}{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e}\mathrel{::}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\};{\delta}

(4)

There must exist
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
such that:

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\};{\delta}

(5)

By definition,
v_{1}=v_{2}=c
such that:

\vdash_{B}c\,\mathrel{::}\,b

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{r[c/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{r[c/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

(6)

We find
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{z==_{b}e}\};{\delta}
, because:

\vdash_{B}c\,\mathrel{::}\,b
by (
5
)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{(z==_{b}e)[c/z]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
because
{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}=c
by (
4
)

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{(z==_{b}e)[c/z]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
because
{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}=c
by (
4
)

TVar

By inversion of the rule and Lemma
B.17
.

TLam

By hypothesis:

(1)

\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x}.~{}e\mathrel{\text{::}}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau

By inversion of the rule we have

(2)

\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau

(3)

\Gamma\vdash\tau_{x}

By inductive hypothesis on (2) we have

(4)

\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}\vdash e\mathrel{\sim}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau

We fix a
\delta
,
v_{x_{1}}
, and
v_{x_{2}}
so that

(5)

\delta\in\Gamma

(6)

v_{x_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{x_{2}}\mathrel{::}\tau_{x};{\delta}

Let
\delta^{\prime}\doteq\delta,(v_{x_{1}},v_{x_{2}})/x
.

By the definition of the logical relation on open terms,
(4), (5), and (6) we have

(7)

{{\delta^{\prime}}_{1}}\cdot{e}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta^{\prime}}_{2}}\cdot{e}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta^{\prime}}

By the definition of substitution

(8)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e[v_{x_{1}}/x]}\mathrel{\sim}{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e[v_{x_{2}}/x]}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta^{\prime}}

By the definition of the logical relation on closed expressions

(9)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e[v_{x_{1}}/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}
,

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e[v_{x_{2}}/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}
,
and
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta^{\prime}}

By the definition and determinism of operational semantics

(10)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x}.~{}e)\ v_{x_{1}}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}
,

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x}.~{}e)\ v_{x_{2}}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}
,
and
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta^{\prime}}

By (6) and the definition of logical relation on function values,

(11)

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x}.~{}e}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x}.~{}e}\mathrel{::}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau;{\delta}

Thus, by the definition of the logical relation,

\Gamma\vdash\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x}.~{}e\mathrel{\sim}\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x}.~{}e\,\mathrel{::}\,{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau

TApp

By hypothesis:

(1)

(16)

So from the definition of logical relations,

\Gamma\vdash e\ e_{x}\mathrel{\sim}e\ e_{x}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau[e_{x}/x]
.

TEqBase

By hypothesis:

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{l}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{l}}\mathrel{::}\tau_{r};{\delta}

.

By (9), (14), and (17) we have

{{\delta^{\prime}}_{1}}\cdot{e}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta^{\prime}}_{2}}\cdot{e}\mathrel{::}\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\};{\delta^{\prime}}

.

By hypothesis

(1)

(8)

(10)

\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\vdash e\mathrel{\sim}e\,\mathrel{::}\,({x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\})

B.14

\Gamma\vdash e_{l}\mathrel{\sim}e_{l}\,\mathrel{::}\,{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau

\delta\in\Gamma
.

(13)

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{l}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{l}}\mathrel{::}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau;{\delta}

(14)

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{r}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{r}}\mathrel{::}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau;{\delta}

By the definition of the logical relation on closed terms:

(15)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{l}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{l_{1}}

(16)

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{l}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{l_{2}}

(17)

v_{l_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{l_{2}}\mathrel{::}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau;{\delta}

(20)

and

and

By which, (15), (20), and Lemma
B.19

(33)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{\texttt{xEq}_{{\tau_{x}}\text{:}{\tau}\rightarrow\ }e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{xEq}_{{\tau_{x}}\text{:}{\tau}\rightarrow\ }v_{l_{1}}\ v_{r_{1}}\ v_{1}

By (16), (20), and (26)

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{\texttt{xEq}_{{\tau_{x}}\text{:}{\tau}\rightarrow\ }e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{xEq}_{{\tau_{x}}\text{:}{\tau}\rightarrow\ }v_{l_{2}}\ v_{r_{2}}\ v_{2}

.

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{\texttt{xEq}_{{\tau_{x}}\text{:}{\tau}\rightarrow\ }e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{\texttt{xEq}_{{\tau_{x}}\text{:}{\tau}\rightarrow\ }e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\delta}
.
