\Gamma\vdash\texttt{xEq}_{{\tau_{x}}\text{:}{\tau}\rightarrow\ }e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\mathrel{\sim}\texttt{xEq}_{{\tau_{x}}\text{:}{\tau}\rightarrow\ }e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\,\mathrel{::}\,\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
.

∎

B.5.
The Logical Relation and the
\mathsf{EqRT}
Type are Equivalence Relations

Theorem B.23 (The logical relation is an equivalence relation).

\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
is reflexive, symmetric, and transivite.

•

Reflexivity: If
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau
, then
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\sim}e\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.

•

Symmetry: If
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
, then
\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\sim}e_{1}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.

•

Transitivity: If
\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau
and
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
and
\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\sim}e_{3}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
,
then
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{3}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.

Proof.

Reflexivity:
This is exactly the Fundamental Property
B.22
.

Symmetry:

Let
\bar{\delta}
be defined such that
\bar{\delta}_{1}(x)=\delta_{2}(x)
and
\bar{\delta}_{2}(x)=\delta_{1}(x)
.
First, we prove that

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
implies

v_{2}\mathrel{\sim}v_{1}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\bar{\delta}}
, by structural induction on
\tau
.

•

\tau\doteq\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}
.
This case is immediate: we have to show that

c\mathrel{\sim}c\mathrel{::}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\};{\bar{\delta}}
given

c\mathrel{\sim}c\mathrel{::}\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\};{\delta}
.
But the definition in this case is itself symmetric: the predicate
goes to
true
under both substitutions.

•

\tau\doteq{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
.
We fix
v_{x_{1}}
and
v_{x_{2}}
so that

(1)

v_{x_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{x_{2}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime}_{x};{\delta}

By the definition of logical relations on open terms and
inductive hypothesis

(2)

v_{x_{2}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{x_{1}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime}_{x};{\bar{\delta}}

By the definition on logical relations on functions

(3)

v_{1}\ v_{x_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\ v_{x_{2}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta,(v_{x_{1}},v_{x_{2}})/x}

By the definition of logical relations on open terms
and since the expressions
v_{1}\ v_{x_{1}}
and
v_{2}\ v_{x_{2}}
are closed,
By the inductive hypothesis on
\tau^{\prime}
:

(4)

v_{2}\ v_{x_{2}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{1}\ v_{x_{1}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\bar{\delta},x:\tau^{\prime}_{x}}

By (2) and the definition of logical relations on open terms

(5)

v_{2}\ v_{x_{2}}\mathrel{\sim}v_{1}\ v_{x_{1}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\bar{\delta},(v_{x_{2}},v_{x_{1}})/x}

By the definition of the logical relation on functions, we conclude
that

v_{2}\mathrel{\sim}v_{1}\mathrel{::}{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime};{\bar{\delta}}

•

\tau\doteq\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
.
By assumption,

(1)

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\delta}

By the definition of the logical relation on
EqRT
types

(2)

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{l}}\mathrel{\sim}{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{r}}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta}

i.e.,
{\delta_{1}}\cdot{(}e_{l})\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{l}
and
similarly for
v_{r}
such that

v_{l}\mathrel{\sim}v_{r}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta}
.

By the IH on
\tau^{\prime}
, we have:

(3)

v_{r}\mathrel{\sim}v_{l}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\bar{\delta}}

And so, by the definition of the LR on equality proofs:

(4)

v_{2}\mathrel{\sim}v_{1}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\bar{\delta}}

Next, we show that
\delta\in\Gamma
implies
\bar{\delta}\in\Gamma
.
We go by structural induction on
\Gamma
.

•

\Gamma=\cdot
. This case is trivial.

•

\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},x:\tau
.
For
x:\tau
, we know that

\delta_{1}(x)\mathrel{\sim}\delta_{2}(x)\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
. By
the IH on
\tau
, we find

\delta_{2}(x)\mathrel{\sim}\delta_{1}(x)\mathrel{::}\tau;{\bar{\delta}}
,
which is just the same as

\bar{\delta}_{1}(x)\mathrel{\sim}\bar{\delta}_{2}(x)\mathrel{::}\tau;{\bar{\delta}}
.
By the IH on
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
we can use similar reasoning to find

\bar{\delta}_{1}(y)\mathrel{\sim}\bar{\delta}_{2}(y)\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\bar{\delta}}

for all
y:\tau^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Now, suppose
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
; we must show
\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\sim}e_{1}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.
We fix
\delta\in\Gamma
; we must show
{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{2}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{1}}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
, i.e., there must exist
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
such that

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}
and

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}
and

v_{2}\mathrel{\sim}v_{1}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.
We have
\delta\in\Gamma
, and so
\bar{\delta}\in\Gamma
by our second lemma. But
then, by assumption, we have
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
such that

{\bar{\delta_{1}}}\cdot{e_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}
and

{\bar{\delta_{2}}}\cdot{e_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}
and

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\bar{\delta}}
. Our first lemma then
yields
v_{2}\mathrel{\sim}v_{1}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
as desired.

Transitivity:

First, we prove an inner property: if
\delta\in\Gamma
and
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
and

v_{2}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
, then
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.
We go by structural induction on the type index
\tau
.

•

\tau\doteq\{{z}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}
.
Here all of the values must be the fixed constant

c
. Furthermore, we must have

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{r[c/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}

and

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{r[c/x]}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{true}
,
so we can immediately find

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.

•

\tau\doteq{x}\text{:}{\tau^{\prime}_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
.

Let
v_{l}\mathrel{\sim}v_{r}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime}_{x};{\delta}
be given.
We must show that
v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta,(v_{l},v_{r})/x}
.
We know by assumption that:

v_{1}\ v_{l}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\ v_{r}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta,(v_{l},v_{r})/x}
and

v_{2}\ v_{l}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\ v_{r}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta,(v_{l},v_{r})/x}
.
By the IH on
\tau^{\prime}
, we find

v_{1}\ v_{l}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\ v_{r}\mathrel{::}\tau^{\prime};{\delta,(v_{l},v_{r})/x}
;
which gives

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta,(v_{l},v_{r})/x}
.

•

\tau\doteq\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
.

To find

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\delta}
,
we merely need to find that

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{l}}\mathrel{\sim}{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{r}}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
,
which we have by inversion on

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\};{\delta}
.

With our proof that the value relation is transitive in hand, we turn our attention to the open relation.
Suppose
\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
and

\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\sim}e_{3}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
; we want to see

\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\sim}e_{3}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
.
Let
\delta\in\Gamma
be given.
We have

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
and

{{\delta}_{1}}\cdot{e_{2}}\mathrel{\sim}{{\delta}_{2}}\cdot{e_{3}}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.
By the definition of the logical relations, we have

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{1}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}
,

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}
,

{\delta_{1}}\cdot{e_{2}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{2}^{\prime}
,

{\delta_{2}}\cdot{e_{3}}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{3}
,

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
, and

v_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.

Moreover, we know that
e_{2}
is well typed, so by the fundamental
theorem (Theorem
B.22
), we know that

\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\sim}e_{2}\,\mathrel{::}\,\tau
, and so

v_{2}\mathrel{\sim}v_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.

By our transitivity lemma on the value relation, we can find that

v_{1}
is equivalent to
v_{2}
is equivalent to
v_{2}^{\prime}
is
equivalent to
v_{3}
, and so

v_{1}\mathrel{\sim}v_{3}\mathrel{::}\tau;{\delta}
.

∎

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{pf}&:&e\rightarrow e\rightarrow\tau\\
\mathsf{pf}(l,r,b)&=&\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}\\
\mathsf{pf}(l,r,{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau)&=&{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\}\\
\end{array}

Our propositional equality
\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
is a
reflection of the logical relation, so it is unsurprising that it is
also an equivalence relation.
We can prove that our propositional equality is treated as an
equivalence relation by the syntactic type system.
There are some tiny wrinkles in the syntactic system: symmetry and
transitivity produce normalized proofs, but reflexivity produces
unnormalized ones in order to generate the correct invariant types

\tau_{l}
and
\tau_{r}
in the base case.

Theorem B.24 (
EqRT
is an equivalence relation).

\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
is reflexive, symmetric, and
transitive on equable types. That is, for all
\tau
that contain
only refinements and functions:

•

Reflexivity: If
\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau
, then
there exists
e_{p}
such that

\Gamma\vdash e_{p}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e\}\ \{e\}
.

•

Symmetry:
\forall\Gamma,\tau,e_{1},e_{2},v_{12}
.
if
\Gamma\vdash v_{12}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
, then
there exists
v_{21}
such that

\Gamma\vdash v_{21}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{2}\}\ \{e_{1}\}
.

•

Transitivity:

\forall\Gamma,\tau,e_{1},e_{2},e_{3},v_{12},v_{23}
.
if
\Gamma\vdash v_{12}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}

and
\Gamma\vdash v_{23}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{2}\}\ \{e_{3}\}
,
then there exists
v_{13}
such that
\Gamma\vdash v_{13}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{3}\}
.

Proof.

Reflexivity
: We strengthen the IH, simultaneously proving
that there exist
e_{p},e_{\mathsf{pf}}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{l}}\ \preceq\ {\tau}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{r}}\ \preceq\ {\tau}
such that

\Gamma,l:\tau_{l},r:\tau_{r}\vdash e_{\mathsf{pf}}\mathrel{\text{::}}\mathsf{pf}(e,e,\tau)
and

\Gamma\vdash e_{p}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e\}\ \{e\}
by induction on

\tau
, leaving
e
general.

•

\tau\doteq\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{e^{\prime}}\}
.

(1)

Let
e_{\mathsf{pf}}=()
.

(2)

Let
e_{p}=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e\ e\ e_{\mathsf{pf}}
.

(3)

Let
\tau_{l}=\tau_{r}=\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{x==_{b}e}\}
.

(4)

We have
{\Gamma}\vdash{x==_{b}e}\ \preceq\ {\tau}
by

SBase
and semantic typing.

(5)

We find
\Gamma\vdash e_{p}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{b}\ \{e\}\ \{e\}
by

TEqBase
, with
e_{l}=e_{r}=e
. We must show:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash e_{l}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{l}
and

\Gamma\vdash e_{r}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{r}
, i.e.,

\Gamma\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{x==_{b}e}\}
;

(b)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{r}}\ \preceq\ {\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{\texttt{true}}\}}
and

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{l}}\ \preceq\ {\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{\texttt{true}}\}}
; and

(c)

\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\vdash e_{\mathsf{pf}}\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}
.

(6)

We find (
5a
) by
TSelf
.

(7)

We find (
5b
) immediately by
SBase
.

(8)

We find (
5c
) by
TVar
, using
TSub
to see that if

l,r:\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{x==_{b}e}\}
then

unit
will be typeable at the refinement where both
l
and
r
are equal to
e
.

•

\tau\doteq{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
.

(1)

\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}\vdash e\ x\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau[x/x]
by
TApp
and
TVar
, noting that
\tau[x/x]=\tau
.

(2)

By the IH on
\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}\vdash e\ x\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau^{\prime}[x/x]=\tau^{\prime}
, there
exist
e_{p}^{\prime},e_{\mathsf{pf}}^{\prime},\tau_{l}^{\prime},
and
\tau_{r}^{\prime}
such that:

(a)

{x:\tau_{x}}\vdash{\tau_{l}^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\tau}
and
{x:\tau_{x}}\vdash{\tau_{r}^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\tau}
;

(b)

\Gamma,x:\tau_{x},l:\tau_{l}^{\prime},r:\tau_{r}^{\prime}\vdash e_{\mathsf{pf}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\mathsf{pf}(e\ x,e\ x,\tau^{\prime})
; and

(c)

\Gamma,x:\tau_{x}\vdash e_{p}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e\ x\}\ \{e\ x\}
.

(3)

If
\tau^{\prime}=\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{\tau^{\prime}}\}{e\ x}{e\ x}
, then

\mathsf{pf}(e\ x,ex,b)=\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{ex==_{b}ex}\}
; otherwise,
\mathsf{pf}(l,r,{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau)={x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e\ x\}\ \{e\ x\}
.

In the former case, let
e_{\mathsf{pf}}^{\prime\prime}=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ (e\ x)(e\ x)e_{\mathsf{pf}}^{\prime}
.
In the latter case, let
e_{\mathsf{pf}}^{\prime\prime}=e_{\mathsf{pf}}^{\prime}
.

Either way, we have
\Gamma,x:\tau_{x},l:\tau_{l}^{\prime},r:\tau_{r}^{\prime}\vdash e_{\mathsf{pf}}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e\ x\}\ \{e\ x\}
by
TEqBase
or
TEqFun
,
respectively.

(4)

Let
e_{\mathsf{pf}}={x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow e_{\mathsf{pf}}^{\prime\prime}
.

(5)

Let
e_{p}=\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e\ e\ e_{\mathsf{pf}}
.

(6)

Let
e_{l}=e_{r}=e
and
\tau_{l}={x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau_{l}^{\prime}
and
\tau_{r}={x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau_{r}^{\prime}
.

(7)

We find subtyping by
SFun
and (
2a
).

(8)

By
TEqFun
. We must show:

(a)

\Gamma\vdash e_{l}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{l}
and
\Gamma\vdash e_{r}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{r}
;

(b)

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{l}}\ \preceq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}
and
{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{r}}\ \preceq\ {{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}
;

(c)

\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\vdash e_{\mathsf{pf}}\mathrel{\text{::}}({x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\})

(d)

\Gamma\vdash{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau

(9)

We find (
8a
) by assumption,
TSub
, and (
7
).

(10)

We find (
8b
) by (
7
).

(11)

We find (
8c
) by
TLam
and (
2b
).

•

\tau\doteq\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
. These types are not equable, so we ignore them.

Symmetry
:
By induction on
\tau
.

•

\tau\doteq\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{e}\}
.

(1)

We have
\Gamma\vdash v_{12}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{b}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
.

(2)

By canonical forms,
v_{12}=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ v_{p}
such that

\Gamma\vdash e_{l}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{l}
and
\Gamma\vdash e_{r}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{r}

(for some
\tau_{l}
and
\tau_{r}
that are refinements of
b
)
and
\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\vdash v_{p}\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}

(Lemma
B.12
).

(3)

Let
v_{21}=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{r}\ e_{l}\ v_{p}
.

(4)

By
TEqBase
, swapping
\tau_{l}
and
\tau_{r}
from
(
2
). We already have appropriate typing and subtyping derivations; we only need to see

\Gamma,l:\tau_{l},r:\tau_{r}\vdash v_{p}\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{r==_{b}l}\}
.

(5)

We have
{\Gamma,l:\tau_{l},r:\tau_{r}}\vdash{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{r==_{b}l}\}}\ \preceq\ {\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}}

by
SBase
and symmetry of
(==_{b})
.

•

\tau\doteq{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
.

(1)

We have
\Gamma\vdash v_{12}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
.

(2)

By canonical forms,
v_{12}=\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime\prime}}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ v_{p}
such that

{\tau_{x}}\vdash{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {a}
nd
{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}}\ \preceq\ {a}
nd

\Gamma\vdash e_{l}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{l}
and
\Gamma\vdash e_{r}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{r}

(for some
\tau_{l}
and
\tau_{r}
that are subtypes of

{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime\prime}
) and
\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l}\vdash v_{p}\mathrel{\text{::}}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\}
.

(3)

By canonical forms, this time on

v_{p}
from (
2
),
v_{p}=\textsc{TLam}{x}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}{e_{p}}
such that

{\Gamma}\vdash{\tau_{x}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{x}^{\prime}}
and
\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l},x:\tau_{x}^{\prime}\vdash e\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that

{\Gamma,r:\tau_{r},l:\tau_{l},x:\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\vdash{\tau^{\prime\prime\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\}}
.

(4)

By
TSub
, (
3
),
and the IH on
\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\}
, we know there
exists some
e_{p}^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma,l:\tau_{l},r:\tau_{r},x:\tau_{x}^{\prime}\vdash e_{p}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime\prime}}\ \{r\ x\}\ \{l\ x\}
.

(5)

Let
v_{p}^{\prime}={x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow e_{p}^{\prime}
.

(6)

By (
4
) and
TLam
, and
TSub
(using subtyping
from (
3
) and (
2
)),

\Gamma,l:\tau_{l},r:\tau_{r}\vdash v_{p}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{r}\ x\}\ \{e_{l}\ x\}
.

(7)

Let
v_{21}=\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{r}\ e_{l}\ v_{p}^{\prime}
.

(8)

By
TEqBase
, with (
6
) for the proof and
(
3
) and (
2
) for the
rest.

•

\tau\doteq\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
. These types are not equable, so we ignore them.

Transitivity
: By induction on
\tau
.

•

\tau\doteq\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{e}\}
.

(1)

We have
\Gamma\vdash v_{12}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
and
\Gamma\vdash v_{23}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{2}\}\ \{e_{3}\}
.

(2)

By canonical forms,

v_{12}=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{1}\ e_{2}\ v_{12}^{\prime}
such that

\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{1}
and

\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{2}
(for some
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
that are refinements of
b
) and

\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1}\vdash v_{12}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}
.
and, similarly,

v_{23}=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{1}\ e_{2}\ v_{23}^{\prime}
such that

\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{2}^{\prime}
and

\Gamma\vdash e_{3}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{3}
(for some
\tau_{2}^{\prime}
and
\tau_{3}
that are refinements of
b
) and

\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime}\vdash v_{23}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}
.

(3)

By canonical forms again, we
know that
v_{12}^{\prime}=v_{23}^{\prime}=\texttt{unit}
and we have:

\begin{array}[]{l}{\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1}}\vdash{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{x==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit}}\}}\ \preceq\ {\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}}\}}\text{, and}\\
{\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime}}\vdash{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{x==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit}}\}}\ \preceq\ {\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}}\}}.\\
\end{array}

(4)

Elaborating on (
3
), we know that
\forall\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1}\right|\!\right]
, we have:

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{x==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit}}\}}\right|\!\right]\subseteq\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}}\right|\!\right]

and
\forall\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime}\right|\!\right]
, we have:

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{x==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit}}\}}\right|\!\right]\subseteq\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}}\right|\!\right].

(5)

Since

{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{x==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit}}\}}
contains
all computations that terminate with
unit
in all models
(Theorem
B.1
), the right-hand sides of
the equations must also hold all unit computations. That is, all choices for

l
and
r_{2}
(resp.
l
and
r
) that are semantically well
typed are necessarily equal.

(6)

By (
5
),
we can infer that in any given model,
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
,
\tau_{2}^{\prime}
,
and
\tau_{3}
identify just one
b
-constant.
Why must
\tau_{2}
and
\tau_{2}^{\prime}
agree? In particular,
e_{2}
has

both
of those types, but by semantic soundness
(Theorem
B.2
), we know that it will go to a
value in the appropriate type interpretation.
By determinism of evaluation, we know it must be the
same
value.
We can therefore conclude that
\forall\theta\in\left[\!\left|\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{1}\right|\!\right]
,

\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{x==_{\texttt{()}}\texttt{unit}}\}}\right|\!\right]\subseteq\left[\!\left|{\theta}\cdot{\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}}\right|\!\right]
.

(7)

By
TEqBase
, using
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{3}
and
unit
as the
proof. We need to show
\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{1}\vdash\texttt{unit}\mathrel{\text{::}}\{{x}\text{:}{\texttt{()}}\mid{l==_{b}r}\}
;
all other premises follow from (
2
).

(8)

By
TSub
and
SBase
, using (
6
) for the subtyping.

•

\tau\doteq{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
.

(1)

We have
\Gamma\vdash v_{12}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
and
\Gamma\vdash v_{23}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{2}\}\ \{e_{3}\}
.

(2)

By canonical forms, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}v_{12}&=&\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{1}\ e_{2}\ v_{12}^{\prime}\\
v_{23}&=&\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{2}\ e_{3}\ v_{23}^{\prime}\\
\end{array}

where there exist types
\tau_{1}
,
\tau_{2}
,
\tau_{2}^{\prime}
, and
\tau_{3}
subtypes of
{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
such that

\begin{array}[]{lr}\Gamma\vdash e_{1}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{1}&\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{2}\\
\Gamma\vdash e_{2}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{2}^{\prime}&\Gamma\vdash e_{3}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{3}\\
\end{array}

and there exist types
\tau_{x_{12}}
,
\tau_{x_{23}}
,
\tau^{\prime}_{12}
, and
\tau^{\prime}_{23}
such that

\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1}\vdash v_{p_{12}}\mathrel{\text{::}}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x_{12}}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}_{12}}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\},\\
{\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1}}\vdash{\tau_{x}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{x_{12}}},\\
{\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1},x:\tau_{x}}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}_{12}}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}},\\
\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime}\vdash v_{p_{23}}\mathrel{\text{::}}{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}_{23}}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\},\\
{\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime}}\vdash{\tau_{x}}\ \preceq\ {\tau_{x_{23}}},\text{ and}\\
{\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime},x:\tau_{x}}\vdash{\tau^{\prime}_{23}}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}}.\end{array}

(3)

By canonical forms on

v_{p_{12}}
and
v_{p_{23}}
from
(
2
), we know that:

v_{p_{12}}=\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x_{12}}.~{}e_{12}^{\prime}\qquad v_{p_{23}}=\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau_{x_{23}}.~{}e_{23}^{\prime}

such that:

\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1},x:\tau_{x_{12}}\vdash e_{12}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{12}^{\prime\prime},\\
{\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1},x:\tau_{x_{12}}}\vdash{\tau_{12}^{\prime\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}_{12}},\\
\\
\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime},x:\tau_{x_{23}}\vdash e_{23}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{23}^{\prime\prime},\\
{\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime},x:\tau_{x_{23}}}\vdash{\tau_{23}^{\prime\prime}}\ \preceq\ {\tau^{\prime}_{23}},\text{ and}\\
\end{array}

(4)

By strengthening
(Lemma
B.7
) using
(
2
), we can replace
x
’s type with
\tau_{x}

in both proofs, to find:

\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1},x:\tau_{x}\vdash e_{12}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{12}^{\prime},\text{and}\\
\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime},x:\tau_{x}\vdash e_{23}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau_{23}^{\prime}.\\
\end{array}

Then, by
TSub
, we can relax the type of the proof bodies:

\begin{array}[]{l}\Gamma,r:\tau_{2},l:\tau_{1},x:\tau_{x}\vdash e_{12}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau^{\prime},\text{and}\\
\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{2}^{\prime},x:\tau_{x}\vdash e_{23}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\tau^{\prime}.\\
\end{array}

(5)

By (
4
,
(
3
), and the IH on

\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\}
, we know there exists some proof body

e_{13}^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma,r:\tau_{3},l:\tau_{1}\vdash e_{13}^{\prime}\mathrel{\text{::}}\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{l\ x\}\ \{r\ x\}
.

(6)

Let
v_{p}={x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow e_{13}^{\prime}
.

(7)

By (
5
), and
TLam
.

(8)

Let
v_{13}=\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{1}\ e_{3}\ v_{p}
.

(9)

By
TEqBase
, with (
7
) for the proof and
(
2
) for the rest.

•

\tau\doteq\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{1}\}\ \{e_{2}\}
. These types are not equable, so we ignore them. ∎

Appendix C
Parallel reduction and cotermination

The conventional application rule for dependent types substitutes a
term into a type, finding
e_{1}~{}e_{2}:\tau[e_{2}/x]

when
e_{1}:{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau
.
We define two logical relations: a unary interpretation of types
(Figure
8
)
and a binary logical relation characterizing equivalence
(Figure
10
).
Both of these logical relations are defined as fixpoints on types.
The type index poses a problem: the function case of these logical
relations quantify over values in the relation, but we sometimes need
to reason about expressions, not values.
If
e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
, are
\tau[e/x]

and
\tau[v/x]
treated the same by our logical relations?
We encounter this problem in particular in proof of logical relation
compositionality, which is precisely about exchanging expressions in
types with the values the expressions reduce to in closing
substitutions: for the unary logical relation and binary logical
relation (Lemma
B.21
).

The key technical device to prove these compositionality lemmas is

parallel reduction
(Figure
13
). Parallel
reduction generalizes our call-by-value relation to allow multiple
steps at once, throughout a term—even under a lambda.
Parallel reduction is a bisimulation
(Lemma
C.5
for forward simulation;
Corollary
C.15
for backward simulation).
That is, expressions that parallel reduce to each other go to
identical constants or expressions that themselves parallel reduce,
and the logical relations put terms that parallel reduce in the same
equivalence class.

To prove the compositionality lemmas, we first show that (a) the
logical relations are closed under parallel reduction ( for
the unary relation and Lemma
B.20
for the
binary relation) and (b) use the backward simulation to change values
in the closing substitution to a substituted expression in the type.

Our proof comes in three steps.
First, we establish some basic properties of parallel reduction (§
C.1
).
Next, proving the forward simulation is straightforward (§
C.2
): if

e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
and
e_{1}\hookrightarrow e_{1}^{\prime}
, then either
parallel reduction contracted the redex for us and

e_{1}^{\prime}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
immediately, or the redex is preserved and

e_{2}\hookrightarrow e_{2}^{\prime}
such that
e_{1}^{\prime}\rightrightarrows e_{2}^{\prime}
.
Proving the backward simulation is more challenging
(§
C.3
). If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
and

e_{2}\hookrightarrow e_{2}^{\prime}
, the redex contracted in
e_{2}
may not yet be exposed.
The trick is to show a tighter bisimulation, where the outermost
constructors are always the same, with the subparts parallel reducing.
We call this relation
congruence
(Figure
14
);
it’s a straightforward restriction of parallel reduction, eliminating

\beta
, eq1, and eq2 as outermost constructors (but allowing them
deeper inside).
The key lemma shows that if
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
, then there
exists
e_{1}^{\prime}

e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{1}^{\prime}
such that

e_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}
(Lemma
C.11
).
Once we know that parallel reduction implies reduction to congruent
terms, proving that congruence is a backward simulation allows us to
reason “up to congruence”. In particular, congruence is a
sub-relation of parallel reduction, so we find that parallel reduction
is a backward simulation.
Finally, we can show that
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
implies
observational equivalence (§
C.4
);
for our purposes, it suffices to find cotermination at constants
(Corollary
C.17
).

One might think, in light of Takahashi’s explanation of parallel
reduction
(Takahashi,
1989
)
, that the simulation
techniques we use are too powerful for our needs: why not simply rely
on the Church-Rosser property and confluence, which she proves quite
simply?
Her approach works well when relating parallel reduction to full

\beta
-reduction (and/or
\eta
-reduction):
the transitive closure of her parallel reduction relation is equal to
the transitive closure of plain
\beta
-reduction (resp.
\eta
- and

\beta\eta
-reduction).
But we’re interested in programming languages, so our underlying
reduction relation isn’t full
\beta
: we use call-by-value, and we
will never reduce under lambdas. But even if we were call-by-name, we
would have the same issue.
Parallel reduction implies reduction, but not to the
same

value, as in her setting. Parallel reduction yields values that are
equivalent,
up to parallel reduction and congruence
(see, e.g.,
Corollary
C.13
).

e\rightrightarrows e

\inference{}{x\rightrightarrows x}[var]\quad\inference{}{c\rightrightarrows c}[const]\quad\inference{\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}\quad e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}}{\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e\rightrightarrows\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime}}[lam]\quad\inference{e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{1}^{\prime}\quad e_{2}\rightrightarrows e_{2}^{\prime}}{e_{1}\ e_{2}\rightrightarrows e_{1}^{\prime}\ e_{2}^{\prime}}[app]

\inference{e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}\quad v\rightrightarrows v^{\prime}}{(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e)\ v\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}[v^{\prime}/x]}[\beta]\quad\inference{}{(==_{b})\ c_{1}\rightrightarrows(==_{(c_{1},b)})}[eq1]\quad\inference{}{(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}\rightrightarrows c_{1}=c_{2}}[eq2]

\inference{e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}\quad e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}\quad e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}}{\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\rightrightarrows\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e^{\prime}}[beq]\quad\inference{\tau_{x}\rightrightarrows\tau_{x}^{\prime}\quad\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}\quad e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}\quad e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}\quad e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}}{\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\rightrightarrows\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e^{\prime}}[xeq]

\tau\rightrightarrows\tau

\inference{r\rightrightarrows r^{\prime}}{\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}\rightrightarrows\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r^{\prime}}\}}[ref]\quad\inference{\tau_{x}\rightrightarrows\tau_{x}^{\prime}\quad\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}}{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau\rightrightarrows{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}[fun]

\inference{\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}\quad e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}\quad e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}}{\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}\rightrightarrows\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}^{\prime}\}\ \{e_{r}^{\prime}\}}[eq]

Figure 13.
Parallel reduction in terms and types.

C.1.
Basic Properties

Lemma C.1 (Parallel reduction is reflexive).

For all
e
and
\tau
,
e\rightrightarrows e
and
\tau\rightrightarrows\tau
.

Proof.

By mutual induction on
e
and
\tau
.

Expressions

•

e\doteq x
. By var.

•

e\doteq c
. By const.

•

e\doteq\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime}
. By the IHs on
\tau
and
e^{\prime}
and lam.

•

e\doteq e_{1}~{}e_{2}
. By the IH on
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
and app.

•

e\doteq\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e^{\prime}
. By the IHs on

e_{l}
,
e_{r}
, and
e^{\prime}
and beq.

•

e\doteq\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e^{\prime}
. By the IHs on

\tau_{x}
,
\tau
,
e_{l}
,
e_{r}
, and
e^{\prime}
and xeq.

Types

•

\tau\doteq\{{x}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}
. By the IH on
r
(an expression) and ref.

•

\tau\doteq{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
. By the IHs on
\tau_{x}
and
\tau^{\prime}
and fun.

•

\tau\doteq\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}
. By the IHs on
\tau^{\prime}
,
e_{l}
, and
e_{r}
and eq.
∎

Lemma C.2 (Parallel reduction is substitutive).

If
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
, then:

(1)

If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
, then
e_{1}[e/x]\rightrightarrows e_{2}[e^{\prime}/x]
.

(2)

If
\tau_{1}\rightrightarrows\tau_{2}
, then
\tau_{1}[e/x]\rightrightarrows\tau_{2}[e^{\prime}/x]
.

Proof.

By mutual induction on
e_{1}
and
\tau_{1}
.

Expressions

var

y\rightrightarrows y
.
If
y\neq x
, then the substitution has no effect and the case is trivial.
If
y=x
, then
x[e/x]=e
and we have
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
by assumption.
We have
e\rightrightarrows e
by reflexivity (Lemma
C.1
).

const

c\rightrightarrows c
. This case is trivial: the substitution has no effect.

lam

\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime}\rightrightarrows\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime\prime}
.
If
y\neq x
, then by the IH on
e^{\prime}
and lam.
If
y=x
, then the substitution has no effect and the case is trivial.

app

e_{11}\ e_{12}\rightrightarrows e_{21}\ e_{22}
, where

e{1i}\rightrightarrows e{2i}
for
i=1,2
.
By the IHs on
e_{1i}
and app.

beta

(\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime})\ v\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}[v^{\prime}/y]
, where

e^{\prime}\rightrightarrows e^{\prime\prime}
and
v\rightrightarrows v^{\prime}
.
If
y\neq x
, then

(\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime}[e/x])\ v[e/x]\rightrightarrows e^{\prime\prime}[e/x][v^{\prime}[e/x]/y]

by
\beta
.
Since
y\neq x
,
e^{\prime\prime}[e/x][v^{\prime}[e/x]/y]=e^{\prime\prime}[v^{\prime}/y][e/x]
as desired.

If
y=x
, then the substitution in the lambda has no effect, and we find

(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime})\ v[e/x]\rightrightarrows e^{\prime\prime}[v^{\prime}[e/x]/x]

by
\beta
.
We have
e^{\prime\prime}[v^{\prime}[e/x]/x]=e^{\prime\prime}[v^{\prime}/x][e/x]
as desired.

eq1

(==_{b})\ c_{1}\rightrightarrows(==_{(c_{1},b)})
. This case is trivial by eq1, as the substitution has no effect.

eq2

(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}\rightrightarrows c_{1}=c_{2}
. This case is trivial by eq2, as the substitution has no effect.

beq

\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e_{p}\rightrightarrows\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e_{p}^{\prime}
, where

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and
e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}
and
e_{p}\rightrightarrows e_{p}^{\prime}
.
By the IHs on
e_{l}
,
e_{r}
, and
e_{p}
and beq.

xeq

\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e_{p}\rightrightarrows\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e_{p}^{\prime}
, where

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and
e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}
and
e_{p}\rightrightarrows e_{p}^{\prime}
.
By the IHs on
e_{l}
,
e_{r}
, and
e_{p}
and xeq.

Types

ref

\{{y}\text{:}{b}\mid{r}\}\rightrightarrows\{{y}\text{:}{b}\mid{r^{\prime}}\}
where
r\rightrightarrows r^{\prime}
.
If
y\neq x
, then
r[e/x]\rightrightarrows r^{\prime}[e^{\prime}/x]
by the IH on
r
; we are done by ref.

If
y=x
, then the substitution has no effect, and the case is immediate by reflexivity (Lemma
C.1
).

fun

{y}\text{:}{\tau_{y}}\rightarrow\tau\rightrightarrows{y}\text{:}{\tau_{y}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}

where
\tau_{y}\rightrightarrows\tau_{y}^{\prime}
and
\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
.
If
y\neq x
, then by the IH on
\tau_{y}
and
\tau
and fun.

If
y=x
, then the substitution only has effect in the domain. The
IH on
\tau_{y}
finds
\tau_{y}[e/x]\rightrightarrows\tau_{y}^{\prime}[e^{\prime}/x]
in
the domain; reflexivity covers the codomain
(Lemma
C.1
), and we are done by fun.

eq

\texttt{PEq}_{\tau}\ \{e_{l}\}\ \{e_{r}\}\rightrightarrows\texttt{PEq}_{\tau^{\prime}}\ \{e_{l}^{\prime}\}\ \{e_{r}^{\prime}\}
.
By the IHs and eq.
∎

Corollary C.3 (Substituting multiple parallel reduction is parallel reduction).

If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows^{*}e_{2}
, then
e[e_{1}/x]\rightrightarrows^{*}e[e{2}/x]
.

Proof.

First, notice that
e\rightrightarrows e
by reflexivity (Lemma
C.1
).
By induction on
e_{1}\rightrightarrows^{*}e_{2}
, using reflexivity in
the base case (Lemma
C.1
); the inductive step uses
substituting parallel reduction (Lemma
C.2
)
and the IH.
∎

Lemma C.4 (Parallel reduction subsumes reduction).

If
e_{1}\hookrightarrow e_{2}
then
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on the evaluation derivation, using reflexivity of
parallel reduction to cover expressions and types that didn’t step (Lemma
C.1
).

ctx

\mathcal{E}[e]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}[e^{\prime}]
, where
e\hookrightarrow e^{\prime}
.
By the IH,
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
.
By structural induction on
\mathcal{E}
.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\bullet
. By the outer IH.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\mathcal{E}_{1}\ e_{2}
. By the inner IH on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, reflexivity on
e_{2}
, and app.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq v_{1}\ \mathcal{E}_{2}
. By reflexivity on
v_{1}
, the inner IH on
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, and app.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ \mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
By reflexivity on
e_{l}
and
e_{r}
, the inner IH on and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, and beq.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ \mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
By reflexivity on
\tau_{x}
,
\tau
,
e_{l}
and
e_{r}
, the inner IH on and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, and xeq.

\beta

(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e)\ v\hookrightarrow e[v/x]
.
By reflexivity (Lemma
C.1
,
e\rightrightarrows e
and
v\rightrightarrows v
.
By beta,
(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e)\ v\rightrightarrows e[v/x]
.

eq1

By eq1.

eq2

By eq2. ∎

C.2.
Forward Simulation

Lemma C.5 (Parallel reduction is a forward simulation).

If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
and
e_{1}\hookrightarrow e_{1}^{\prime}
, then
there exists
e_{2}^{\prime}
such that
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}^{\prime}
and

e_{1}^{\prime}\rightrightarrows e_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
e_{1}\hookrightarrow e_{1}^{\prime}
, leaving
e_{2}
general.

ctx

By structural induction on
\mathcal{E}
, using reflexivity (Lemma
C.1
) on parts where the IH doesn’t apply.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\bullet
. By the outer IH on the actual step.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\mathcal{E}_{1}\ e_{2}
. By the IH on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, reflexivity on
e_{2}
, and app.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq v_{1}\ \mathcal{E}_{2}
. By reflexivity on
v_{1}
, the IH on
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, and app.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ \mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
By reflexivity on
e_{l}
and
e_{r}
, the IH on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, and beq.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ \mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
By reflexivity on
\tau_{x}
,
\tau
,
e_{l}
and
e_{r}
, the IH on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, and xeq.

\beta

(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e)\ v\hookrightarrow e[v/x]
.
One of two rules could have applied to find
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
: app or
\beta
.

In the app case, we have
e_{2}=(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime})\ v^{\prime}

where
\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
and
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
and

v\rightrightarrows v^{\prime}
.
Let
e_{2}^{\prime}=e^{\prime}[v^{\prime}/x]
.
We find
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}^{\prime}
in one step by
\beta
.
We find
e[v/x]\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}[v^{\prime}/x]
by
substitutivity of parallel reduction (Lemma
C.2
).

In the
\beta
case, we have
e_{2}=e^{\prime}[v^{\prime}/x]
such
that
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
and
v\rightrightarrows v^{\prime}
.
Let
e_{2}^{\prime}=e_{2}
.
We find
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}^{\prime}
in no steps at all;
we find
e_{1}^{\prime}\rightrightarrows e_{2}^{\prime}
by substitutivity of parallel
reduction (Lemma
C.2
).

eq1

(==_{b})\ c_{1}\hookrightarrow(==_{(c_{1},b)})
.
One of two rules could have applied to find

(==_{b})\ c_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
: app or eq1.

In the app case, we must have
e_{2}=e_{1}=(==_{b})\ c_{1}
, because there are no reductions
available in these constants.
Let
e_{2}^{\prime}=(==_{(c_{1},b)})
.
We find
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}^{\prime}
in a single step by our assumption (or eq1).
We find parallel reduction by reflexivity (Lemma
C.1
).

In the eq2 case, we have
e_{2}=e_{1}^{\prime}=(==_{(c_{1},b)})
. Let
e_{2}^{\prime}=e_{2}
.
We find
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}^{\prime}
in no steps at all.
We find parallel reduction by reflexivity (Lemma
C.1
).

eq2

(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}\hookrightarrow c_{1}=c_{2}
.
One of two rules could have applied to find

(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
: app or eq2.

In the app case, we have
e_{2}=e_{1}={(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}}
, because there are no
reductions available in these constants.
Let
e_{2}^{\prime}\doteq c_{1}=c_{2}
, i.e.
true
when
c_{1}=c_{2}
and
false
otherwise.
We find
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}^{\prime}
in a single step by our assumption (or eq2).
We find parallel reduction by reflexivity (Lemma
C.1
).

In the eq2 case, we have
e_{2}=e_{1}^{\prime}\doteq c_{1}=c_{2}
, i.e.
true
when
c_{1}=c_{2}
and
false
otherwise.
Let
e_{2}^{\prime}=e_{2}
.
We find
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}^{\prime}
in no steps at all.
We find parallel reduction by reflexivity
(Lemma
C.1
).
∎

C.3.
Backward Simulation

Lemma C.6 (Reduction is substitutive).

If
e_{1}\hookrightarrow e_{2}
, then
e_{1}[e/x]\hookrightarrow e_{2}[e/x]
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
e_{1}\hookrightarrow e_{2}
.

ctx

By structural induction on
\mathcal{E}
.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\bullet
. By the outer IH.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\mathcal{E}_{1}\ e_{2}
. By the IH on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and ctx.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq v_{1}\ \mathcal{E}_{2}
. By the IH on
\mathcal{E}_{2}
and ctx.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ \mathcal{E}^{\prime}
. By the IH on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and ctx.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ \mathcal{E}^{\prime}
. By the IH on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and ctx.

\beta

(\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime})\ v\hookrightarrow e^{\prime}[v/y]
.
We must show

(\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime})[e/x]\ v[e/x]\hookrightarrow e^{\prime}[v/y][e/x]
.

The exact result depends on whether
y=x
.
If
y\neq x
, the substitution goes through, and we have
(\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime})[e/x]=\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau[e/x].~{}e^{\prime}[e/x]
.
By
\beta
,

(\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau[e/x].~{}e^{\prime}[e/x])\ v[e/x]\hookrightarrow e^{\prime}[e/x][v[e/x]/y]
.
But
e^{\prime}[e/x][v[e/x]/y]=e^{\prime}[v/y][e/x]
, and we are done.

If, on the other hand,
y=x
, then the substitution has no effect in
the body of the lambda, and
(\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime})[e/x]=\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau[e/x].~{}e^{\prime}
.
By
\beta
again, we find

(\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau[e/x].~{}e^{\prime})\ v[e/x]\hookrightarrow e^{\prime}[v[e/x]/y]
.
Since
y=x
, we really have

e^{\prime}[v[e/x]/x]
which is the same as

e^{\prime}[v/x][e/x]=e^{\prime}[v/y][e/x]
, as desired.

eq1

The substitution has no effect; immediate, by eq1.

eq2

The substitution has no effect; immediate, by eq2. ∎

Corollary C.7 (Multi-step reduction is substitutive).

If
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}
, then
e_{1}[e/x]\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}[e/x]
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{2}
. The
base case is immediate (
e_{1}=e_{2}
, and we take no
steps). The inductive case follows by the IH and single-step
substitutivity (Lemma
C.6
).
∎

\inference{}{x\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}x}[var]\quad\inference{}{c\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}c}[const]\quad\inference{\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}\quad e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}}{\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime}}[lam]\quad\inference{e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{1}^{\prime}\quad e_{2}\rightrightarrows e_{2}^{\prime}}{e_{1}\ e_{2}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{1}^{\prime}\ e_{2}^{\prime}}[app]

\inference{e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}\quad e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}\quad e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}}{\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e^{\prime}}[beq]\quad\inference{\tau_{x}\rightrightarrows\tau_{x}^{\prime}\quad\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}\quad e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}\quad e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}\quad e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}}{\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e^{\prime}}[xeq]

Figure 14.
Term congruence.

We say terms are
congruent
when they (a) have the same
outermost constructor and (b) their subparts parallel reduce to each
other.
4
4
4
Congruent terms are related to Takahashi’s
\tilde{M}

operator: in that they characterize parallel reductions that preserve
structure. They are not the same, though: Takahashi’s
\tilde{M}

will do
\beta\eta
-reductions on outermost redexes.

That is,
\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\subseteq\rightrightarrows
, where the outermost
rule must be one of var, const, lam, app, beq, or xeq and cannot be a

real
reduction like
\beta
, eq1, or eq2.

Congruence is a key tool in proving that parallel reduction is a
backward simulation.
Parallel reductions under a lambda prevent us from having an
“on-the-nose” relation, but reduction can keep up enough with
parallel reduction to maintain congruence.

Lemma C.8 (Congruence implies parallel reduction).

If
e_{1}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}
then
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
e_{1}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}
.

var

x\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}x
. By var.

const

c\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}c
. By const.

lam

\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime}
, with

\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
and
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
. By lam.

app

e_{1}\ e_{2}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{1}^{\prime}\ e_{2}^{\prime}
, with

e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{1}^{\prime}
and
e_{2}\rightrightarrows e_{2}^{\prime}
. By
app.

beq

\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e
,
with
e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and
e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}

and
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
.
By beq.

xeq

By xeq.

\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e
,
with
\tau_{x}\rightrightarrows\tau_{x}^{\prime}
and
\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
and

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and
e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}
and

e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
.
By xeq.
∎

We need to strengthen substitutivity (Lemma
C.2
) to
show that it preserves congruence.

Corollary C.9 (Congruence is substitutive).

If
e_{1}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{1}^{\prime}
and
e_{2}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}^{\prime}
,
then

e_{1}[e_{2}/x]\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}[e_{2}^{\prime}/x]
.

Proof.

By cases on
e_{1}
.

•

e_{1}=y
.
It must be that
e_{2}=y
as well, since only var could have applied.
If
y\neq x
, then the substitution has no effect and we have
y\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}y
by assumption (or var).
If
x=y
, then
e_{1}[e_{2}/x]=e_{2}
and we have
e_{2}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}^{\prime}
by assumption.

•

e_{1}=c
.
It must be that
e_{2}=c
as well.
The substitution has no effect; immediate by var.

•

e_{1}=\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e
.
It must be that
e_{2}=\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime}
such that

\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
and
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
.
If
y\neq x
, then we must show

\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau[e_{2}/x].~{}e[e_{2}/x]\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}[e_{2}^{\prime}/x].~{}e^{\prime}[e_{2}^{\prime}/x]
,
which we have immediately by lam and Lemma
C.2

on our two subparts.
If
y=x
, then we must show

\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau[e_{2}/x].~{}e\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\lambda y\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}[e_{2}^{\prime}/x].~{}e^{\prime}
,
which we have immediately by lam, Lemma
C.2
on
our
\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
, and the fact that

e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
.

•

e_{1}=e_{11}\ e_{12}
.
It must be that
e_{2}=e_{21}\ e_{22}
, such that

e_{11}\rightrightarrows e_{21}
and

e_{12}\rightrightarrows e_{22}
.
By app and Lemma
C.2
on the subparts.

•

e_{1}=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e
.
It must be the case that
e_{2}=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e^{\prime}
where

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and
e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}
.
By beq and Lemma
C.2
on the subparts.

•

e_{1}=\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e
.
It must be the case that
e_{2}=\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e^{\prime}
where

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
(and similarly for
\tau_{x}
,
\tau
,

e_{r}
, and
e
).
By xeq and Lemma
C.2
on the subparts.
∎

Lemma C.10 (Parallel reduction of values implies congruence).

If
v_{1}\rightrightarrows v_{2}
then
v_{1}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
v_{1}\rightrightarrows v_{2}
.

var

Contradictory: variables aren’t values.

const

Immediate, by const.

lam

Immediate, by lam.

app

Contradictory: applications aren’t values.

beq

Immediate, by beq.

xeq

Immediate, by xeq.

\beta

Contradictory: applications aren’t values.

eq1

Contradictory: applications aren’t values.

eq2

Contradictory: applications aren’t values. ∎

Lemma C.11 (Parallel reduction implies reduction to congruent forms).

If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
, then there exists
e_{1}^{\prime}

e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{1}^{\prime}
such that
e_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
.

Structural rules

var

x\rightrightarrows x
. We have
e_{1}=e_{2}=x
by var.

const

c\rightrightarrows c
. We have
e_{1}=e_{2}=c
by const.

lam

\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e\rightrightarrows\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime}
,
where
\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
and
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
. Immediate, by lam.

app

e_{11}\ e_{12}\rightrightarrows e_{21}\ e_{22}
,
where
e_{11}\rightrightarrows e_{21}
and
e_{12}\rightrightarrows e_{22}
.
Immediate, by app.

beq

\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\rightrightarrows\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e^{\prime}

where
e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and
e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}

and
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
. Immediate, by beq.

xeq

\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e\rightrightarrows\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ e^{\prime}

where
\tau_{x}\rightrightarrows\tau_{x}^{\prime}
and
\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
and

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and
e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}
and

e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
. Immediate, by xeq.

Reduction rules

These are the more interesting cases, where the parallel reduction
does a reduction step—ordinary reduction has to do more work to
catch up.

\beta

(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e)\ v\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}[v^{\prime}/x]
,
where
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime\prime}
and
v\rightrightarrows v^{\prime\prime}
.

We have
(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e)\ v\hookrightarrow e[v/x]
by
\beta
.
By the IH on
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime\prime}
, there exists
e^{\prime}
such
that
e\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}
such that
e^{\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e^{\prime\prime}
.
We
ignore
the IH on
v\rightrightarrows v^{\prime\prime}
, noticing instead
that parallel reducing values are congruent
(Lemma
C.10
) and so

v\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v^{\prime\prime}
.
Since reduction is substitutive
(Corollary
C.7
), we can find that

e[v/x]\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}[v/x]
.
Since congruence is substitutive (Lemma
C.9
),
we have
e^{\prime}[v/x]\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e^{\prime\prime}[v^{\prime\prime}/x]
,
as desired.

eq1

(==_{b})\ c_{1}\rightrightarrows(==_{(c_{1},b)})
.
We have

(==_{b})\ c_{1}\hookrightarrow(==_{(c_{1},b)})

in a single step; we find congruence by const.

eq2

(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}\rightrightarrows c_{1}=c_{2}
.
We have
(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}\hookrightarrow c_{1}=c_{2}
in a single step; we find congruence by const.
∎

Lemma C.12 (Congruence to a value implies reduction to a value).

If
e\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v^{\prime}
then
e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
such that

v\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on
v^{\prime}
.

•

v^{\prime}\doteq c
. It must be the case that
e=c
. Let
v=c
. By const.

•

v^{\prime}\doteq\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime\prime}
. It must be the case
that
e=\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e^{\prime}
such that

\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
and
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime\prime}
. By lam.

•

v\doteq\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ v_{p}^{\prime}
. It must
be the case that
e=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e_{p}
where

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and

e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}
and

e_{p}\rightrightarrows v_{p}^{\prime}
.
Since parallel reduction implies reduction to congruent forms
(Lemma
C.11
), we have

e_{p}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{p}^{\prime}
and
e_{p}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v_{p}^{\prime}
.
By the IH on
v_{p}^{\prime}
, we know that
e_{p}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{p}

such that
v_{p}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v_{p}^{\prime}
.
By repeated use of ctx, we find

\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e_{p}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ v_{p}
.
Since its proof part is a value, this term is a value.
We find

\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ v_{p}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ v_{p}^{\prime}

by ebeq.

•

v\doteq\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ v_{p}^{\prime}
. It must
be the case that
e=\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e_{p}
where

\tau_{x}\rightrightarrows\tau_{x}^{\prime}
and

\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
and

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and

e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}
and

e_{p}\rightrightarrows v_{p}^{\prime}
.
Since parallel reduction implies reduction to congruent forms
(Lemma
C.11
), we have

e_{p}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{p}^{\prime}
and
e_{p}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v_{p}^{\prime}
.
By the IH on
v_{p}^{\prime}
, we know that
e_{p}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{p}

such that
v_{p}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v_{p}^{\prime}
.
By repeated application of ctx, we find

\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e_{p}\hookrightarrow^{*}\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ v_{p}
.
Since its proof part is a value, this term is a value.
We find

\texttt{xEq}_{{\tau_{x}}\text{:}{\tau}\rightarrow\ }e_{l}\ e_{r}\ v_{p}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ v_{p}^{\prime}

by exeq.
∎

Corollary C.13 (Parallel reduction to a value implies reduction to a related value).

If
e\rightrightarrows v^{\prime}
then there exists
v
such that

e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
and
v\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Since parallel reduction implies reduction to congruent forms
(Lemma
C.11
), we have
e\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}

such that
e^{\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v^{\prime}
.
But congruence to a value implies reduction to a value
(Lemma
C.12
), so
e^{\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}v
such
that
v\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v^{\prime}
.
By transitivity of reduction,
e\hookrightarrow^{*}v
.
∎

Lemma C.14 (Congruence is a backward simulation).

If
e_{1}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}
and
e_{2}\hookrightarrow e_{2}^{\prime}
then
there exists
e_{1}^{\prime}
where
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{1}^{\prime}
such that

e_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
e_{2}\hookrightarrow e_{2}^{\prime}
.

ctx

\mathcal{E}[e]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}[e^{\prime}]
, where
e\hookrightarrow e^{\prime}
.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\bullet
. By the outer IH.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\mathcal{E}_{1}\ e_{2}
.
It must be that
e_{1}=e_{11}\ e_{12}
, where

e_{11}\rightrightarrows\mathcal{E}_{1}[e]
and

e_{12}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
.
By the IH on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, finding evaluation with ctx and congruence
with app.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq v_{1}^{\prime}\ \mathcal{E}_{2}
.
It must be that
e_{1}=e_{11}\ e_{12}
, where

e_{11}\rightrightarrows v_{1}^{\prime}
and

e_{12}\rightrightarrows\mathcal{E}_{2}[e_{2}]
.
We find that
e_{11}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{1}
such that

v_{1}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v_{1}^{\prime}
by Corollary
C.13
.
By the IH on
\mathcal{E}_{2}
and evaluation with ctx and congruence with
app.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ \mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
It must be the case that
e_{1}=\texttt{bEq}_{b}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e_{p}
where

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and
e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}
.
By the IH on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
; we find the evaluation with ctx and congruence
with beq.

–

\mathcal{E}\doteq\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}}\ e_{l}^{\prime}\ e_{r}^{\prime}\ \mathcal{E}^{\prime}
.
It must be the case that
e_{1}=\texttt{xEq}_{{x}\text{:}{\tau_{x}}\rightarrow\tau}\ e_{l}\ e_{r}\ e_{p}
such that

\tau_{x}\rightrightarrows\tau_{x}^{\prime}
and
\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}
and

e_{l}\rightrightarrows e_{l}^{\prime}
and
e_{r}\rightrightarrows e_{r}^{\prime}
.
By the IH on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
; we find the evaluation with ctx and congruence
with xeq.

\beta

(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime})\ v^{\prime}\hookrightarrow e^{\prime}[v^{\prime}/x]
.
Congruence implies that
e_{1}=e_{11}\ e_{12}
such that

e_{11}\rightrightarrows\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime}
and

e_{12}\rightrightarrows v^{\prime}
.
Parallel reduction to a value implies reduction to a congruent value
(Corollary
C.13
),
e_{11}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{11}

such that
v_{11}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau^{\prime}.~{}e^{\prime}
, i.e.,

v_{11}=\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e
such that
\tau\rightrightarrows\tau^{\prime}

and
e\rightrightarrows e^{\prime}
.
Similarly,
e_{12}\hookrightarrow^{*}v
such that
v\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}v^{\prime}
.

By
\beta
, we find
(\lambda x\mathord{:}\tau.~{}e)\ v\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}[v/x]
;
by transitivity of reduction, we have
e_{1}=e_{11}\ e_{12}\hookrightarrow^{*}e^{\prime}[v/x]
.
Since congruence is substitutive (Corollary
C.9
), we have

e[v/x]\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e^{\prime}[v^{\prime}/x]
.

eq1

(==_{b})\ c_{1}\hookrightarrow(==_{(c_{1},b)})
.
Congruence implies that
e_{1}=e_{11}\ e_{12}
such that

e_{11}\rightrightarrows(==_{b})
and

e_{12}\rightrightarrows c_{1}
.
Parallel reduction to a value implies reduction to a related value
(Corollary
C.13
),
e_{11}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{11}

such that
v_{11}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}(==_{b})
(and similarly
for
e_{12}
and
c_{1}
).
But the each constant is congruent only to itself, so

v_{11}=(==_{b})
and
v_{12}=c_{1}
.
We have
(==_{b})\ c_{1}\hookrightarrow(==_{(c_{1},b)})
by assumption.
So
e_{1}=e_{11}\ e_{12}\hookrightarrow^{*}(==_{(c_{1},b)})
by
transitivity, and we have congruence by const.

eq2

(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}\hookrightarrow c_{1}=c_{2}
.
Congruence implies that
e_{1}=e_{11}\ e_{12}
such that

e_{11}\rightrightarrows(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}
and

e_{12}\rightrightarrows c_{2}
.
Parallel reduction to a value implies reduction to a related value
(Corollary
C.13
),
e_{11}\hookrightarrow^{*}v_{11}

such that
v_{11}\rightrightarrows(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}
(and similarly
for
e_{12}
and
c_{2}
).
But the each constant is congruent only to itself, so

v_{11}=(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}
and
v_{12}=c_{2}
.
We have
(==_{(c_{1},b)})\ c_{2}\hookrightarrow c_{1}=c_{2}
already, by assumption.
So
e_{1}=e_{11}\ e_{12}\hookrightarrow^{*}c_{1}=c_{2}
by
transitivity, and we have congruence by const.
∎

Corollary C.15 (Parallel reduction is a backward simulation).

If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
and
e_{2}\hookrightarrow e_{2}^{\prime}
, then
there exists
e_{1}^{\prime}
such that
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{1}^{\prime}
and

e_{1}^{\prime}\rightrightarrows e_{2}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Parallel reduction implies reduction to congruent forms, so

e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{1}^{\prime}
such that
e_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}
.
But congruence is a backward simulation (Lemma
C.14
), so
e_{1}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{1}^{\prime\prime}
such that
e_{1}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 10.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color\hskip 5.0pt\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color}\raisebox{0.0pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 5.0pt}}\raisebox{-3.77881pt}{\leavevmode\hbox{\set@color$\rightsquigarrow$}}\hskip 10.0pt}}e_{2}^{\prime}
.
By transitivity of evaluation,
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}e_{1}^{\prime\prime}
.
Finally, congruence implies parallel reduction
(Lemma
C.8
), so
e_{1}^{\prime\prime}\rightrightarrows e_{2}^{\prime}
,
as desired.
∎

C.4.
Cotermination

Theorem C.16 (Cotermination at constants).

If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows e_{2}
then
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}c
iff
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}c
.

Proof.

By induction on the evaluation steps taken, using direct reduction in
the base case (Corollary
C.13
) and using parallel
reduction as a forward and backward simulation
(Lemmas
C.5
and Corollary
C.15
) in the inductive case.
∎

Corollary C.17 (Cotermination at constants (multiple parallel steps)).

If
e_{1}\rightrightarrows^{*}e_{2}
then
e_{1}\hookrightarrow^{*}c
iff
e_{2}\hookrightarrow^{*}c
.

Proof.

By induction on the parallel reduction derivation. The base case is
immediate (
e_{1}=e_{2}
); the inductive case follows from
cotermination at constants (Theorem
C.16
) and the
IH.
∎
